# Seven strongest rules for building and teaching

Finalized 2026-10-08 after [the collected-source compilation audit](../../research/karpathy-brain-2026-10-08/COMPILATION-AUDIT.md). These seven recurring patterns are a synthesis of reviewed public evidence, not Karpathy's endorsement, private mind or a claim that every historical implementation followed them. Each has two distinct original source artifacts with exact quotation support. Separate artifacts corroborate recurring advice; they do not establish independent experimental replication. Automatic-caption wording remains uncorrected and is not audio-verified.

**Evidence** below describes his advice or demonstration. **Application** is our practical implementation and grading standard. Repository ownership, model-generated prompts, third-party contributions and syndicated copies were not used as sole proof of personal authorship. Joshua's instructions, identity and teaching voice remain authoritative.

## 1. Explain and reconstruct the mechanism

**Evidence:** His study guidance distinguishes active reconstruction from passive understanding; his backprop article recommends implementation to solidify mechanism-level understanding.

> Reading and understanding IS NOT the same as replicating

[Study advice](../sources/karpathy/blog/undergraduate-study-advice.md), study section; [exact raw text](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/blog/md/other-first-party/cs.stanford.edu_people_karpathy_advice.html.md). Publication date unknown.

> get you to write backprop manually and help you solidify your understanding.

[Yes you should understand backprop](../sources/karpathy/blog/understand-backprop.md), conclusion, 2016-12-19; [exact raw text](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/blog/md/medium/karpathy.medium.com_yes-you-should-understand-backprop-e2f06eab496b.md).

**Application:** Explain input, transformation and output using a small example, then invite the learner to reproduce or explain one step. Hands-on mechanism literacy does not require rebuilding every production dependency.

## 2. Start with one correct small case and a baseline

**Evidence:** His recipe recommends getting a specific implementation working before generalizing. In Makemore he starts the neural pipeline with one word rather than the full dataset.

> get that to work, and then generalize it later making sure that I get the same result.

[Recipe](../sources/karpathy/blog/recipe.md), “generalize a special case,” 2019-04-25; [exact raw text](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/blog/md/github-blog/karpathy.github.io_2019_04_25_recipe.md).

> [01:06:43] all the words just yet because I want
> [01:06:45] everything to be manageable. Uh so,
> [01:06:47] let's just do the first word, which is

[Makemore lecture](../sources/karpathy/lectures/pacmpygffxo.md), 2022-09-07; [exact raw captions](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/youtube/transcripts/PaCmpygFfXo.md). The preceding cue says not to take all words yet.

**Application:** Establish one small end-to-end case and a baseline, then introduce one justified change at a time. A teaching toy and a production system have different success requirements. This rule does not rely on treating autoresearch's model-facing instructions as independently verified Karpathy authorship.

## 3. Predict, run and compare

**Evidence:** His recipe describes hypotheses followed by validation or investigation; Makemore predicts normalized row sums and unchanged results before running the vectorized calculation.

> make concrete hypotheses about what will happen and then either validate them with an experiment or investigate until we find some issue.

[Recipe](../sources/karpathy/blog/recipe.md), “The recipe,” 2019-04-25; [exact raw text](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/blog/md/github-blog/karpathy.github.io_2019_04_25_recipe.md).

> [00:44:04] taking its sum. We expect this to be
> [00:44:06] one.

[Makemore lecture](../sources/karpathy/lectures/pacmpygffxo.md), normalization check, 2022-09-07; [exact raw captions](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/youtube/transcripts/PaCmpygFfXo.md). At 00:44:16 he runs the calculation; 00:44:19 reports the same result.

**Application:** State the expected behavior before executing a meaningful check, report what happened, and explain any mismatch. Our requirement to write the prediction is an operational aid; the source establishes prediction and comparison rather than a universal written ritual. Expected output never substitutes for observed output.

## 4. Expose a counterexample and repair its cause

**Evidence:** Micrograd exposes a repeated-node derivative bug with a tiny counterexample; the backprop article traces why clipping a forward error accidentally eliminates the needed gradient.

> [01:22:39] so here's the simplest case that shows
> [01:22:42] the bug

[Micrograd lecture](../sources/karpathy/lectures/vmj-3s1tku0.md), repeated-node example, 2022-08-16; [exact raw captions](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/youtube/transcripts/VMj-3S1tku0.md). It expects derivative two, exposes overwritten contributions and repairs them with accumulation.

> The problem is on line 291.

[Backprop article](../sources/karpathy/blog/understand-backprop.md), DQN clipping example, 2016-12-19; [exact raw text](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/blog/md/medium/karpathy.medium.com_yes-you-should-understand-backprop-e2f06eab496b.md). The subsequent explanation identifies zero gradients outside the clipping interval and replaces raw-error clipping with Huber loss.

**Application:** Use a meaningful edge or failing case to make a cause inspectable, repair it and check the repaired behavior. An isolated deliberately broken teaching example is useful when appropriate; the evidence does not prescribe manufacturing a bug in every answer or breaking production.

## 5. Verify the relevant behavior, not merely a successful run

**Evidence:** His coding notes recommend tests with explicit success criteria. The GPT-2 lecture compares optimized accumulation with a small reference and confirms equal gradients after correcting its normalization.

# Teach with small mechanisms and inspectable checks

The [collected sources are compiled](../../research/karpathy-brain-2026-10-08/COMPILATION-AUDIT.md). [Seven rules](rules.md) have exact quotes from two distinct original artifacts each. They are practical synthesis, with historical exceptions retained. Joshua's instructions and teaching voice remain authoritative.

**Reconstruct the mechanism.** “Reading and understanding IS NOT the same as replicating”. [Study advice](../sources/karpathy/blog/undergraduate-study-advice.md).

**Begin with a small baseline.** “get that to work, and then generalize it later making sure that I get the same result.” [Recipe](../sources/karpathy/blog/recipe.md).

**Predict, run and compare.** “make concrete hypotheses about what will happen and then either validate them with an experiment or investigate until we find some issue.” [Recipe](../sources/karpathy/blog/recipe.md).

**Repair a cause through a counterexample.** “The problem is on line 291.” [Backprop article](../sources/karpathy/blog/understand-backprop.md), the clipped-error gradient example.

**Verify relevant behavior.** “Get it to write tests first and then pass them.” [Coding notes](../sources/karpathy/x/claude-coding-notes.md). Running code alone does not establish correctness; [rules](rules.md) preserves a contrasting historical beam-search admission.

**Expose assumptions and uncertainty.** “the models make wrong assumptions on your behalf and just run along with them without checking.” [Coding notes](../sources/karpathy/x/claude-coding-notes.md). “with double digit percent uncertainty” [Animals vs Ghosts](../sources/karpathy/animals-vs-ghosts.md).

**Earn complexity.** “I cannot simplify this any further.” [Microgpt](../sources/karpathy/blog/microgpt.md). Necessary production safeguards still belong in production.

The configured teaching agent reads this page first, uses at most five wiki pages, cites source pages and applies all seven rules. The skill grades its answer before presentation. End with **What I ran**, **What came out**, **What I changed**. Teaching code belongs outside the vault in `ai-os-projects/karpathy-method-tools/examples/`.

Joshua selected Codex for this method. The fresh [tokenizer lesson](../../research/karpathy-brain-2026-10-08/CODEX-TOKENIZER-ACCEPTANCE.md) passed seven tests with current-file receipts. The [Codex hook](../../research/karpathy-brain-2026-10-08/CODEX-STOP-ACCEPTANCE.md) passed 36 tests and its six definitions are Trusted/enabled. Native main Stop blocked a scoped edit, recovered after execution and left an unrelated turn alone. Child SubagentStop is not separately live-tested. The [fresh native lesson](../../research/karpathy-brain-2026-10-08/NATIVE-CODEX-LESSON-ACCEPTANCE.md) verified registered-agent discovery, real handoff and independent parent grading. Use $karpathy-teach in Codex; its parser rejects the original slash spelling. Generic fallback is no longer permitted by the skill. Claude quota is historical.

Use existing `ingest` for new sources. Preserve raw evidence. Foreign authors, payloads, caption errors and linked media retain their limits. [Index](index.md), [principles](principles.md), [methods](methods.md), [current status](../../research/karpathy-video-2026-10-08/VERBATIM-COMPLIANCE.md).

#!/bin/sh
# Validate the skills DB model (claude/ + codex/ subtrees, activations, INDEX).
# Logic lives in check-skills.py; this wrapper keeps the historic entrypoint.
# POSIX sh on purpose: must behave identically under `sh`, `bash`, and `zsh`.

SCRIPT_DIR=$(CDPATH= cd -- "$(dirname -- "$0")" && pwd -P)
ROOT=$(CDPATH= cd -- "$SCRIPT_DIR/../.." && pwd -P)

Tool result:
# Skills — master database

This is the **master database** for skills across Joshua's whole setup — every authored, project, and vendor/plugin skill from every root (Claude, Codex, plugins, per-project `.claude/` and `.agents/`), unioned in one place.

## Storage model

- **Inert.** Nothing here auto-loads. A skill only runs when it's *activated* — **copied** (not symlinked, since 2026-07-17) into a project's `.claude/skills/` or `.agents/skills/` (or into the conservative global roots). This directory is search-and-storage, not a load path. A copy doesn't auto-follow edits to the DB source — re-copy after editing a skill here; `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` flags any activation whose `SKILL.md` has drifted.
- **Split by tool origin, always.** Skills are filed under `claude/` or `codex/` depending on which tool's root they came from — **never merged into one shared folder, even when the content is byte-identical.** If a skill exists for both tools, it gets one entry under each: `claude/<name>/` and `codex/<name>/`. This keeps each tool's set independently editable without silently forking a shared file.
- **A+B for vendor/plugin skills.** A vendor or plugin skill gets (A) a live symlink under `claude/<name>/` or `codex/<name>/` pointing at its real location in the plugin, so it always reflects the installed version, **and** (B) a frozen copy under `claude/_snapshots/<name>/` or `codex/_snapshots/<name>/` for point-in-time reference. Snapshots are refreshed manually, not on a schedule — re-run the refresh only when asked.
- **Authored and project skills** are real files, source of truth, filed the same way under `claude/<name>/` or `codex/<name>/`.

## Save-to-both

Creating a new skill (via skill-creator or by hand) always writes to **both**: the current project's `.claude/skills/` (or `.agents/skills/`) **and** this DB, every time, unconditionally. New skills are project-scoped by default — never auto-promoted to global.

## Finding things

- `claude/README.md` and `codex/README.md` index that tool's skills.
- `INDEX.md` (once generated) is the full one-line-per-skill index across both, grouped by source-type.

See [PARITY.md](PARITY.md) for runtime decisions and [the decision log](../decisions/log.md) for the consolidation rationale.

### Authored / project skills

- **a11y-debugging** — Uses Chrome DevTools MCP for accessibility (a11y) debugging and auditing based on web.dev guidelines. Use when testing semantic HTML, ARIA labels, focus states, keyboard navigation, tap targets, and color contrast.
- **academy-guide** — Stop and check this skill before finishing any reply to a question about how to use Claude or a Claude product — it recommends matching courses, tutorials, and use cases from Claude Academy (academy.claude.com), Anthropic's learning hub. Trigger on: "how do I", "how can I", "getting started with", "what can Claude do", "teach me", "learn to use"; questions about artifacts, projects, skills, plugins, connectors, MCP; requests about rolling Claude out to a team, class, or organization; and any ask for training materials, onboarding content, or learning resources. Use it when the user is learning how to use a feature or product — not when they are mid-task and just want the task done. This skill composes with other skills: after consulting product documentation to answer how a Claude feature works, also check here for a matching course or tutorial — a docs-grounded answer and an Academy recommendation belong together. Only recommend on a strong match; never invent Academy content.
- **accommodations-email** — Send Joshua's LSAT accommodations email ("Request for Accommodations", LSAC Qualified Professional Form PDF attached) to one or more students. Use whenever Joshua mentions accommodations together with a person, in any wording: "send the accommodations email to X", "accommodations for X", "send X the accommodations", "X needs accommodations", "email X about extra time", "send X the LSAC form", "run/execute/do the accommodations skill for X", "accommodations email to X and Y". Dictation often hears "skill" as "scale", so "the accommodations scale" means this skill. Pre-approved template: naming the students is the go-ahead to send. Sends and logs itself.

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Validate the skills DB model (replaces the old 'expect exactly 11' check).

Activation model (changed 2026-07-17, Joshua's call): a project activates a DB skill
by COPYING it into `.claude/skills/<name>/` (or `.agents/skills/`), not symlinking.
Copies are real files — portable, committable, no dead links on another machine.
The tradeoff: a copy does not auto-follow DB edits. This script's (e) check exists
because of that tradeoff — it flags copies that have drifted from their DB source.
Symlinks are still tolerated (not an error) for any that predate the change or for
the unrelated vendor-plugin mechanism in the DB itself (skills/claude/_snapshots
pattern), which this script does not touch.

Checks:
  (a) every activation in an active root resolves to real content (no broken symlink);
  (b) no skill lives in an active root un-mirrored (real dir with no DB counterpart);
  (c) INDEX.md covers every top-level DB skill;
  (d) no duplicate canonical name within a subtree;
  (e) a copied (non-symlink) activation's SKILL.md matches its DB source byte-for-byte
      — catches silent drift after the DB is edited but the activation isn't re-synced.
  (f) every multi-segment path cited in CLAUDE.md / AGENTS.md resolves on disk
      — routing maps that point at moved or deleted files send sessions hunting.
  (g) no UNDECLARED claude/codex asymmetry. The two subtrees are intentionally unequal,
      so the legitimate split is declared in skills/PARITY.md and only new drift fails.
  (h) effective Claude/Codex skill availability has no undeclared one-runtime drift;
  (i) active instruction scopes have both CLAUDE.md and AGENTS.md;
  (j) Codex agent TOML files are valid, complete, and activated from the catalog.
Exit 0 = PASS, 1 = FAIL.
"""
from project_paths import project_path, VAULT_ROOT
import os, re, sys, filecmp, tomllib, subprocess
from pathlib import Path

BRAIN = str(Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2])
DB = os.path.join(BRAIN, "skills")
HOME = str(Path.home())
SPECIAL = {"_snapshots", "_project-forks", "_inactive", "README.md", "INDEX.md"}

# Active roots to audit (only those that exist are checked).
PROJECT_ACTIVE_ROOTS = [
    os.path.join(BRAIN, ".claude", "skills"),
    os.path.join(BRAIN, ".agents", "skills"),
    os.path.join(BRAIN, ".gemini", "skills"),
    str(project_path("email-hq", ".claude", "skills")),
]

# Home-level roots also contain skills installed and managed by external plugins.
# They are included when comparing effective Claude/Codex availability, but they
# are not required to be mirrored into this project's catalog.
RUNTIME_ACTIVE_ROOTS = {
    "claude": [os.path.join(HOME, ".claude", "skills"),
               os.path.join(BRAIN, ".claude", "skills")],
    "codex": [os.path.join(HOME, ".agents", "skills"),
              os.path.join(BRAIN, ".agents", "skills")],
}

def db_names(sub):
    base = os.path.join(DB, sub)
    if not os.path.isdir(base):
        return set()
    return {n for n in os.listdir(base)
            if n not in SPECIAL and (os.path.isdir(os.path.join(base, n)) or os.path.islink(os.path.join(base, n)))}


def entrypoints_match(first, second):
    """Resolve valid local links so documented relocation is not content drift."""
    first, second = Path(first), Path(second)
    a = first.read_text(encoding="utf-8").replace("\r\n", "\n")
    b = second.read_text(encoding="utf-8").replace("\r\n", "\n")
    if a == b:
        return True
    pattern = r"\]\(([^)]+)\)"
    left, right = list(re.finditer(pattern, a)), list(re.finditer(pattern, b))
    if len(left) != len(right):
        return False
    if re.sub(pattern, "](<link>)", a) != re.sub(pattern, "](<link>)", b):
        return False
    for x, y in zip(left, right):
        t1, t2 = x.group(1), y.group(1)
        if t1 == t2:
            continue
        if any(re.match(r"[a-zA-Z][a-zA-Z0-9+.-]*:", t) for t in (t1, t2)):
            return False
        p1, _, anchor1 = t1.partition("#")
        p2, _, anchor2 = t2.partition("#")
        r1, r2 = (first.parent / p1).resolve(), (second.parent / p2).resolve()
        if anchor1 != anchor2 or r1 != r2 or not r1.exists():
            return False
    return True


def owned_provider_errors(roots):
    """Check two maintained provider references, not arbitrary plugin payloads."""
    errors = []
    for root in roots:
        for name in ("claude-api", "academy-guide"):
            path = Path(root) / name / "SKILL.md"
            if not path.is_file():
                continue
            text = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
            if name == "claude-api":
                match = re.search(r"(?mi)^name:\s*([^\r\n]+)", text)
                if match and match.group(1).strip().strip("\"'") != "claude-api":
                    errors.append(f"provider identity mismatch: {path}")
                if re.search(r"(?i)\bCodex(?:-| )opus\b|\bCodex API / Anthropic SDK", text):
                    errors.append(f"mixed Codex/Anthropic identity: {path}")
            if name == "academy-guide" and re.search(r"(?i)academy\.codex\.com", text):
                errors.append(f"corrupted Anthropic Academy URL: {path}")
    return errors

def main():
    fails = []
    # DB structure
    for sub in ("claude", "codex", "gemini"):
        if not os.path.isdir(os.path.join(DB, sub)):
            fails.append(f"DB subtree missing: skills/{sub}")
    claude_db = db_names("claude")
    codex_db = db_names("codex")
    gemini_db = db_names("gemini")

    # (d) duplicate canonical names within a subtree — filesystem guarantees uniqueness,
    # but validate SKILL.md name matches dir name where a SKILL.md exists.
    for sub, names in (("claude", claude_db), ("codex", codex_db), ("gemini", gemini_db)):
        for n in names:
            # Vendor entries are symlinks whose dir name may be disambiguated
            # (e.g. discord-access -> plugin skill named 'access'); skip name check.
            if os.path.islink(os.path.join(DB, sub, n)):
                continue
            real = os.path.realpath(os.path.join(DB, sub, n))
            skf = os.path.join(real, "SKILL.md")
            if os.path.isfile(skf):
                with open(skf, encoding="utf-8", errors="replace") as f:
                    head = f.read(1200)
                m = re.search(r"(?mi)^name:\s*(.+)$", head)
                if m and m.group(1).strip().strip('"').strip("'") != n:
                    fails.append(f"name mismatch: skills/{sub}/{n} declares '{m.group(1).strip()}'")

    # (a)+(b) active roots
    all_db = claude_db | codex_db | gemini_db
    for root in PROJECT_ACTIVE_ROOTS:
        if not os.path.isdir(root):
            continue
        for n in os.listdir(root):
            if n.startswith(".") or n in SPECIAL:
                continue
            p = os.path.join(root, n)
            if os.path.islink(p):
                if not os.path.exists(p):
                    fails.append(f"broken activation symlink: {root}/{n}")
                elif n not in all_db:
                    fails.append(f"activation not represented in DB: {root}/{n} -> {os.path.realpath(p)}")
            elif os.path.isdir(p):
                # real dir in an active root: must be mirrored in DB by name
                if n not in all_db:
                    fails.append(f"un-mirrored skill in active root: {root}/{n} (not in DB)")
                    continue
                # (e) drift check: copied activation's SKILL.md vs its DB source
                parts = Path(root).parts
                sub = "gemini" if ".gemini" in parts else "codex" if ".agents" in parts else "claude"
                db_dir = os.path.join(DB, sub, n)
                act_skf = os.path.join(p, "SKILL.md")
                db_skf = os.path.join(db_dir, "SKILL.md")
                if not os.path.isfile(db_skf):
                    fails.append(f"activation missing runtime DB source: {root}/{n} -> skills/{sub}/{n}")
                if os.path.isfile(act_skf) and os.path.isfile(db_skf):
                    if not entrypoints_match(act_skf, db_skf):
                        fails.append(f"stale activation copy: {root}/{n} differs from skills/{sub}/{n} — re-copy from DB")

    # (c) INDEX coverage
    idx = os.path.join(DB, "INDEX.md")
    if not os.path.isfile(idx):
        fails.append("INDEX.md missing")
    else:
        with open(idx, encoding="utf-8") as f:
            itext = f.read()
        indexed = set(re.findall(r"^- \*\*(?:[a-z0-9-]+: )?([^*]+)\*\*", itext, re.M))
        for sub, names in (("claude", claude_db), ("codex", codex_db), ("gemini", gemini_db)):
            for n in names:
                if n not in indexed:
                    fails.append(f"INDEX missing DB skill: {sub}/{n}")

    # (f) dead paths in the routing maps. Only multi-segment paths (a/b) are checked;
    # bare fragments like `codex/` are prose, not routing targets.
    remote_ids = set()
    remote = subprocess.run(["git", "-C", BRAIN, "remote", "get-url", "origin"], capture_output=True, text=True)
    match = re.search(r"github\.com[:/]([^/]+/[^/\s]+?)(?:\.git)?$", remote.stdout.strip())
    if match:
        remote_ids.add(match.group(1))
    for fname in ("CLAUDE.md", "AGENTS.md"):
        fpath = os.path.join(BRAIN, fname)
        if not os.path.isfile(fpath):
            fails.append(f"routing file missing: {fname}")
            continue
        with open(fpath, encoding="utf-8") as f:
            text = f.read()
        for cand in sorted(set(re.findall(r"`([^`\s]+/[^`\s]+)`", text))):
            if cand.startswith("http") or cand in remote_ids or any(c in cand for c in "<>*{}|"):
                continue
            p = cand.rstrip("/")
            if p.startswith("~"):
                p = os.path.expanduser(p)
            ap = p if p.startswith("/") else os.path.join(BRAIN, p)
            if not os.path.exists(ap):
                fails.append(f"dead path in {fname}: `{cand}`")

    # (g) undeclared claude/codex asymmetry, measured against skills/PARITY.md.
    parity = os.path.join(DB, "PARITY.md")
    if not os.path.isfile(parity):
        fails.append("PARITY.md missing (skill asymmetry baseline)")
    else:
        with open(parity, encoding="utf-8") as f:
            ptext = f.read()
        # A bare "- name" list cannot carry the reason each split needs, and a
        # baseline without reasons is a snapshot rather than a decision (that is
        # exactly how the 2026-07-19 baseline went unaudited). So names are read
        # from `backticks` anywhere in the file, which lets each entry sit beside
        # its justification in prose. Only tokens that name a real skill count —
        # that ignores the incidental backticks (paths, flags, MCP servers)
        # without needing them escaped, and a typo'd name simply fails to
        # declare, surfacing as undeclared drift rather than passing silently.
        known = claude_db | codex_db
        declared = {t for t in re.findall(r"`([A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]*)`", ptext)
                    if t in known}
        pairs = {}
        pair_file = Path(DB) / "runtime-pairs.toml"
        if pair_file.exists():
            pairs = tomllib.loads(pair_file.read_text(encoding="utf-8")).get("pairs", {})
            for claude_name, codex_name in pairs.items():
                if claude_name not in claude_db or codex_name not in codex_db:
                    fails.append(f"missing paired runtime skill: {claude_name} -> {codex_name}")
                else:
                    declared.update((claude_name, codex_name))
        for n in sorted((claude_db - codex_db) - declared):
            fails.append(f"undeclared parity drift: skills/claude/{n} has no codex counterpart "
                         f"(activate for both, or declare in skills/PARITY.md)")
        for n in sorted((codex_db - claude_db) - declared):
            fails.append(f"undeclared parity drift: skills/codex/{n} has no claude counterpart "
                         f"(activate for both, or declare in skills/PARITY.md)")

        # (h) effective runtime parity. Project activations can satisfy a skill
        # absent from the home-level root and vice versa. External plugin skills
        # do not have to live in this DB, but unexplained one-runtime availability
        # is still drift worth surfacing.
        effective = {}
        for runtime, roots in RUNTIME_ACTIVE_ROOTS.items():
            names = set()
            for root in roots:
                if os.path.isdir(root):
                    names.update(
                        n for n in os.listdir(root)
                        if not n.startswith(".")
                        and os.path.isfile(os.path.join(root, n, "SKILL.md"))
                    )
            effective[runtime] = names
        for claude_name, codex_name in pairs.items():
            if (claude_name in effective["claude"]) != (codex_name in effective["codex"]):
                fails.append(f"incomplete paired activation: Claude {claude_name} / Codex {codex_name}")
        for n in sorted((effective["claude"] - effective["codex"]) - declared):
            fails.append(f"undeclared activation drift: {n} is available to Claude but not Codex")
        for n in sorted((effective["codex"] - effective["claude"]) - declared):
            fails.append(f"undeclared activation drift: {n} is available to Codex but not Claude")

    # (i) instruction twins. Frozen, imported, and vendor reference material is
    # not an active project scope and is intentionally excluded.
    ignored_parts = {".git", "archive", "graph-imports", "node_modules", "raw", "reference", "_snapshots", "anthropic-capture"}
    for current, dirs, files in os.walk(BRAIN):
        dirs[:] = [d for d in dirs if d not in ignored_parts]
        present = set(files)
        if "CLAUDE.md" in present and "AGENTS.md" not in present:
            fails.append(f"missing AGENTS.md peer: {os.path.relpath(current, BRAIN)}")
        if "AGENTS.md" in present and "CLAUDE.md" not in present:
            fails.append(f"missing CLAUDE.md peer: {os.path.relpath(current, BRAIN)}")

    # (j) project-scoped Codex agents.
    agent_catalog = os.path.join(BRAIN, "agents", "codex")
    agent_active = os.path.join(BRAIN, ".codex", "agents")
    if os.path.isdir(agent_catalog):
        for name in sorted(n for n in os.listdir(agent_catalog) if n.endswith(".toml")):
            source = os.path.join(agent_catalog, name)
            try:
                with open(source, "rb") as handle:
                    data = tomllib.load(handle)
            except (OSError, tomllib.TOMLDecodeError) as exc:
                fails.append(f"invalid Codex agent TOML: agents/codex/{name}: {exc}")
                continue
            for field in ("name", "description", "developer_instructions"):
                if not data.get(field):
                    fails.append(f"Codex agent missing {field}: agents/codex/{name}")
            activated = os.path.join(agent_active, name)
            if not os.path.isfile(activated):
                fails.append(f"Codex agent not activated: .codex/agents/{name}")
            elif not filecmp.cmp(source, activated, shallow=False):
                fails.append(f"stale Codex agent activation: .codex/agents/{name}")

    # Owned global provider sanity is deliberately bounded; external packages
    # need not be catalogued or automatically mirrored to satisfy this check.
    fails.extend(owned_provider_errors([
        Path(HOME) / ".agents/skills", Path(HOME) / ".codex/skills",
        Path(HOME) / ".claude/skills",
    ]))

    if fails:
        for msg in fails:
            print(f"skills: FAIL — {msg}", file=sys.stderr)
        print(f"skills: FAIL ({len(fails)} problems)", file=sys.stderr)
        sys.exit(1)
    print(f"skills: PASS (claude={len(claude_db)} codex={len(codex_db)} gemini={len(gemini_db)} DB skills; "
          f"activations resolve; INDEX complete)")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

or >\")\n    broken = []\n    for target in local_links(body):\n        if not target:\n            continue\n        p = (skf.parent / target)\n        if not p.exists():\n            broken.append(target)\n    if broken:\n        rec[\"broken_links\"] = broken[:20]\n        rec[\"issues\"].append(f\"{len(broken)} broken relative link(s)\")\n    return rec\n\n\ndef main():\n    out = []\n    for key, root in ROOTS.items():\n        if not root.is_dir():\n            continue\n        for entry in sorted(os.listdir(root)):\n            if entry.startswith(\".\") or entry in SKIP or entry.endswith(\".md\") or entry.endswith(\".toml\"):\n                continue\n            p = root / entry\n            if not (p.is_dir() or p.is_symlink()):\n                continue\n            out.append(scan_skill(key, root, entry))\n    # plugin skills\n    cache = HOME / \".claude\" / \"plugins\" / \"cache\"\n    if cache.is_dir():\n        for skf in cache.rglob(\"SKILL.md\"):\n            d = skf.parent\n            rec = scan_skill(\"claude:plugin\", d.parent, d.name)\n            rec[\"plugin_path\"] = str(d.relative_to(cache))\n            out.append(rec)\n    json.dump(out, sys.stdout, indent=1, default=str)\n\n\nmain()\n"}

  consultation session transcripts belong to transcript-intake, not this pipeline.

# Skills repaired with useful workflows preserved

Implemented 2026-10-10 against the [completed audit](../skills-2026-10-09/SUMMARY.md), using the installed Karpathy agent and independent review. Corrected actionable skill instructions and supporting code, then reconciled actual catalog and activation files. Two legacy input dependencies remain unavailable, listed below.

## Highest-risk repairs came first

| Problem | Result |
|---|---|
| Provider names, SDK identity and hardcoded model defaults were confused | Anthropic implementation stays Anthropic; Codex host instructions use actual host tools. Model availability and signatures require current evidence. Explicit provider/model choices are preserved. |
| Reviews could miss unstaged/untracked work | Review modes explicitly distinguish committed, working-tree and combined scope. Isolated Git fixtures exercise the previous omission. |
| Skills could stage unrelated changes, delete work or imply sending/publication | Scope and existing authorization are respected; staging stays bounded; drafts, reviews and intake do not imply sending, publishing or deletion. Useful workflows remain available. |
| Broad triggers intercepted unrelated tasks | API skills require their provider; hosted YouTube references require that provider; ordinary captions stay free; chat export requires an actual file-export request; persona creation, intake, query, lint and handoff retain distinct purposes. |
| Old paths, copied activations and literal unavailable host tools | Current-root routing and supported host entrypoints replace known invalid calls. Compatibility aliases remain thin wrappers and are catalogued. Existing Gemini-specific skills are catalogued without adding Claude/Codex/global activations. |
| Video helpers could delete frames or upload audio automatically | Fresh output directories required; existing frames/audio preserved; FFmpeg uses no-overwrite; paid audio transcription requires an explicit provider. Missing captions/keys remain visible. Missing Codex-global watch payload installed in the already-existing activation. |
| MCP examples used unsafe HTML and weak filesystem containment | User data rendered as text nodes; resolved filesystem targets checked against the registered root. Isolated JavaScript fixtures verify these mechanisms; this is not real-browser or adversarial race certification. |
| Graphify incremental imports could collide or retain stale data | Basename collisions stop visibly; changed/deleted sources require rebuilding. Graphify's actual remote/model pipeline was not exercised. |
| Verification tracer timed out resolving every executed line | Filename resolution happens once per filename; current execution receipts complete. Existing hook adapter regression tests pass. Hook trust/configuration was not changed. |

Originals, extra useful payloads, raw sources and historical snapshots are preserved. No files were deleted, no emails/messages sent, no paid provider called, and no commit, push or publication performed. Existing unrelated working-tree edits were not reset.

## Verification describes what actually ran

- `check-skills.py` passes: Claude 224, Codex 211, Gemini 222 catalog entries. Project activations resolve and match their runtime catalog; the generated index is complete.
- YAML parses for 1,732 current catalog/activation/global entrypoints, excluding inactive/historical project forks. Four malformed descriptions were quoted without changing their meaning.
- 900 distinct repaired or added skill/support/validator/override files are reconciled, with current hashes and original backups where a file already existed. This count is physical files, not 900 distinct skills.
- 84 direct regression checks and 30 earlier literal checks pass. All 649 original catalog dispositions remain accounted for. All 74 frozen snapshot hashes are unchanged.
- Nine video-boundary fixture tests, four validator/generator fixture tests and two MCP scaffold mechanism tests pass. The original/fixed comparisons cover relevant faults; these are isolated local tests, not live account operations.

claude_last = {}\ncodex = collections.Counter(); codex_last = {}\nskill_call = re.compile(r'\"name\":\"Skill\",\"input\":\\{\"skill\":\"([^\"]+)\"')\nfiles = list((HOME / \".claude\" / \"projects\").rglob(\"*.jsonl\"))\nfor f in files:\n    try:\n        text = f.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\")\n    except OSError:\n        continue\n    for m in skill_call.finditer(text):\n        claude[m.group(1)] += 1\n        claude_last[m.group(1)] = max(claude_last.get(m.group(1), \"\"), f.stat().st_mtime.__str__())\nread_skill = re.compile(r'skills[\\\\/]+([a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]*)[\\\\/]+SKILL\\.md')\ncfiles = list((HOME / \".codex\" / \"sessions\").rglob(\"*.jsonl\")) if (HOME / \".codex\" / \"sessions\").is_dir() else []\nfor f in cfiles:\n    try:\n        text = f.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\")\n    except OSError:\n        continue\n    seen = set(m.group(1) for m in read_skill.finditer(text))\n    for s in seen:\n        codex[s] += 1  # sessions that read this SKILL.md at least once\njson.dump({\"claude_files\": len(files), \"codex_files\": len(cfiles),\n           \"claude\": claude.most_common(), \"codex_sessions\": codex.most_common()},\n          open(\"usage.json\", \"w\"), indent=1)\nprint(\"claude transcripts\", len(files), \"distinct skills invoked\", len(claude))\nprint(\"codex sessions\", len(cfiles), \"distinct skills read\", len(codex))\nprint(\"CLAUDE top 40:\", claude.most_common(40))\nprint(\"CODEX top 60:\", codex.most_common(60))\nEOF\npython usage.py", "description": "Count skill usage across all Claude and Codex session logs", "timeout": 600000}

{"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os\\38a93a63-e397-478e-9df6-b7085bedd58f\\scratchpad\\usage_all.py", "content": "\"\"\"Skill usage across every local Claude and Codex session log.\n\nSignals:\n  claude_skill  - Claude Skill tool invocations (\"name\":\"Skill\" ... \"skill\":\"x\")\n  claude_read   - Claude sessions that Read a skills/<x>/SKILL.md (non-bulk sessions only)\n  codex_read    - Codex sessions that read a skills/<x>/SKILL.md (non-bulk sessions only)\n  claude_meter  - ~/.claude.json skillUsage lifetime counter (includes /slash invocations)\nA session that touches more than BULK distinct SKILL.md files is an audit/inventory run, not use.\n\"\"\"\nimport collections\nimport json\nimport re\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nHOME = Path.home()\nBULK = 10\nSKILL_CALL = re.compile(r'\"name\":\"Skill\",\"input\":\\{\"skill\":\"([^\"]+)\"')\n# JSON-escaped Windows paths look like skills\\\\name\\\\SKILL.md; POSIX paths skills/name/SKILL.md.\nSKILL_PATH = re.compile(r'skills(?:\\\\\\\\|\\\\|/)+([a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]*)(?:\\\\\\\\|\\\\|/)+SKILL\\.md')\n\n\ndef scan(root, count_calls):\n    calls, reads = collections.Counter(), collections.Counter()\n    sessions = bulk = 0\n    for f in root.rglob(\"*.jsonl\"):\n        sessions += 1\n        text = f.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\")\n        if count_calls:\n            for m in SKILL_CALL.finditer(text):\n                calls[m.group(1).split(\":\")[-1]] += 1\n        seen = {m.group(1) for m in SKILL_PATH.finditer(text)}\n        if len(seen) > BULK:\n            bulk += 1\n            continue\n        for name in seen:\n            reads[name] += 1\n    return sessions, bulk, calls, reads\n\n\nc_sessions, c_bulk, c_calls, c_reads = scan(HOME / \".claude\" / \"projects\", True)\nx_sessions, x_bulk, _, x_reads = scan(HOME / \".codex\" / \"sessions\", False)\nmeter = {k.split(\":\")[-1]: v[\"usageCount\"]\n         for k, v in json.load(open(HOME / \".claude.json\", encoding=\"utf-8\"))[\"skillUsage\"].items()}\n\nnames = set(c_calls) | set(c_reads) | set(x_reads) | set(meter)\ntable = {n: {\"claude_skill\": c_calls.get(n, 0), \"claude_read\": c_reads.get(n, 0),\n             \"codex_read\": x_reads.get(n, 0), \"claude_meter\": meter.get(n, 0)} for n in sorted(names)}\njson.dump({\"claude_sessions\": c_sessions, \"claude_bulk\": c_bulk, \"codex_sessions\": x_sessions,\n           \"codex_bulk\": x_bulk, \"table\": table}, open(\"usage_all.json\", \"w\"), indent=1)\nprint(f\"Claude sessions {c_sessions} (bulk excluded {c_bulk}); Codex sessions {x_sessions} (bulk excluded {x_bulk})\")\nprint(f\"distinct skills with any use signal: {len(names)}\")\ntop = sorted(table.items(), key=lambda kv: -sum(kv[1].values()))\nfor n, v in top[:45]:\n    print(f\"{sum(v.values()):>4}  {n:<34} {v}\")\n"}

{"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os\\38a93a63-e397-478e-9df6-b7085bedd58f\\scratchpad\\usage_all.py", "content": "\"\"\"Skill usage across every local Claude and Codex session log.\n\nOnly deliberate actions count:\n  claude_skill  - Claude Skill tool invocations\n  claude_open   - Claude tool_use lines (Read/Bash/etc.) whose input opens skills/<x>/SKILL.md\n  codex_open    - Codex function_call/custom_tool_call lines whose arguments open skills/<x>/SKILL.md\n  claude_meter  - ~/.claude.json skillUsage lifetime counter (includes /slash invocations)\nEach open counts once per session. Sessions opening more than BULK distinct skills are audits.\nMentions in injected context (skill listings, hook output, instructions) are ignored because\nonly tool-call lines are inspected.\n\"\"\"\nimport collections\nimport json\nimport re\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nHOME = Path.home()\nBULK = 10\nSKILL_CALL = re.compile(r'\"name\":\"Skill\",\"input\":\\{\"skill\":\"([^\"]+)\"')\nSKILL_PATH = re.compile(r'skills(?:\\\\\\\\|\\\\|/)+([a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]*)(?:\\\\\\\\|\\\\|/)+SKILL\\.md')\n\n\ndef scan(root, is_claude):\n    calls, opens = collections.Counter(), collections.Counter()\n    sessions = bulk = 0\n    for f in root.rglob(\"*.jsonl\"):\n        sessions += 1\n        seen = set()\n        with open(f, encoding=\"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\") as handle:\n            for line in handle:\n                if is_claude:\n                    if '\"type\":\"tool_use\"' not in line:\n                        continue\n                    for m in SKILL_CALL.finditer(line):\n                        calls[m.group(1).split(\":\")[-1]] += 1\n                    # only the tool_use input, not tool results or text\n                    for chunk in re.findall(r'\"type\":\"tool_use\".*?\"input\":(\\{.*?\\})', line):\n                        seen.update(m.group(1) for m in SKILL_PATH.finditer(chunk))\n                else:\n                    if '\"function_call\"' not in line and '\"custom_tool_call\"' not in line:\n                        continue\n                    if '\"function_call_output\"' in line or '\"custom_tool_call_output\"' in line:\n                        continue\n                    seen.update(m.group(1) for m in SKILL_PATH.finditer(line))\n        if len(seen) > BULK:\n            bulk += 1\n            continue\n        for name in seen:\n            opens[name] += 1\n    return sessions, bulk, calls, opens\n\n\nc_sessions, c_bulk, c_calls, c_opens = scan(HOME / \".claude\" / \"projects\", True)\nx_sessions, x_bulk, _, x_opens = scan(HOME / \".codex\" / \"sessions\", False)\nmeter = {k.split(\":\")[-1]: v[\"usageCount\"]\n         for k, v in json.load(open(HOME / \".claude.json\", encoding=\"utf-8\"))[\"skillUsage\"].items()}\n\nnames = set(c_calls) | set(c_opens) | set(x_opens) | set(meter)\ntable = {n: {\"claude_skill\": c_calls.get(n, 0), \"claude_open\": c_opens.get(n, 0),\n             \"codex_open\": x_opens.get(n, 0), \"claude_meter\": meter.get(n, 0)} for n in sorted(names)}\njson.dump({\"claude_sessions\": c_sessions, \"claude_bulk\": c_bulk, \"codex_sessions\": x_sessions,\n           \"codex_bulk\": x_bulk, \"table\": table}, open(\"usage_all.json\", \"w\"), indent=1)\nprint(f\"Claude sessions {c_sessions} (bulk excluded {c_bulk}); Codex sessions {x_sessions} (bulk excluded {x_bulk})\")\nprint(f\"distinct skills with any deliberate use: {len(names)}\")\n\n\ndef score(v):\n    return max(v[\"claude_skill\"], v[\"claude_meter\"]) + v[\"claude_open\"] + v[\"codex_open\"]\n\n\nfor n, v in sorted(table.items(), key=lambda kv: -score(kv[1])):\n    print(f\"{score(v):>4}  {n:<34} {v}\")\n"}

Tool result:
# Skill parity baseline

## 2026-10-09: Persona Agent Creator

`persona-agent-creator` is catalogued and activated as matching project copies in both runtimes. Its host-aware factory procedure requires actual native person-agent registration and an independently grading entry skill. It reuses existing ingest/schema conventions, derives each person's own rules, and explicitly tests new hook scope instead of inheriting Karpathy's hardcoded guard. Codex invokes `$persona-agent-creator`; generated person agents use each host's native configuration. No global activation was added.

Declared single-runtime skills. `check-skills.py` fails on **undeclared** asymmetry only:
when you deliberately add a skill to one runtime and not the other, add it here in the same
commit. When a skill shows up as undeclared drift, either activate it for both runtimes or
declare it here **with a reason**.

Every entry below carries its justification. An entry without one is not a decision, it is a
snapshot — which is what this file was until the audit noted below.

**Audited 2026-09-12.** The Windows parity pass repaired the cross-platform validator,
catalogued `kling-cli` for both runtimes, and ported 25 previously undeclared Claude-only
skills to Codex: `academy-guide`, `algorithmic-art`, `brand-guidelines`, `canvas-design`,
`claude-api`, `claude-handoff`, `code-review`, `discernment-nudge`, `doc-coauthoring`,
`docx`, `implement-spec`, `internal-comms`, `loop-me`, `mcp-builder`,
`migrate-to-shoehorn`, `pdf`, `pptx`, `retro`, `session-recap`,
`setup-ts-deep-modules`, `slack-gif-creator`, `theme-factory`,
`web-artifacts-builder`, `wizard`, and `xlsx`. Current catalog counts:
**claude=211 codex=188**.

Note that DB parity is not activation. The DB is the complete catalog; Codex's activated set
in `.agents/skills/` is a deliberately small ops core. Porting a skill here does not activate
it, and is not meant to.

## Claude only

### Vendor / plugin snapshots (24) — not authored here

`a11y-debugging`, `agent-development`, `build-mcp-app`, `build-mcp-server`, `build-mcpb`,
`chrome-devtools`, `claude-automation-recommender`, `claude-md-improver`,
`command-development`, `debug-optimize-lcp`, `discord-access`, `discord-configure`,
`example-command`, `example-skill`, `hook-development`, `imessage-access`,
`imessage-configure`, `math-olympiad`, `mcp-integration`, `playground`, `plugin-settings`,
`plugin-structure`, `skill-development`, `troubleshooting`

These live under `skills/claude/_snapshots/` and ship with Claude plugins. They document
Claude's own extension surfaces (hooks, plugins, MCP authoring) or vendor integrations. A
Codex copy would describe machinery Codex does not have. Correct as Claude-only, permanently.

### Claudex package: mirrored runtime directions since 2026-09-30

Joshua authorized universal activation and an inverse Codex version of every skill in chaseai-yt/claudex-loop. All six package workflows are available in both catalogs and project/global activation roots. Codex uses claude-review, claude-build, grill-me-claude, and grill-with-docs-claude; Claude retains codex-review, codex-build, grill-me-codex, and grill-with-docs-codex. These deliberate name differences are paired equivalents, not missing skills. Their SKILL.md entry points deliberately differ by runtime; shared upstream supporting files remain complete.

| Skill | Claude host | Codex host |
|---|---|---|
| claudex-loop | Claude plans, Codex reviews; host builds by default | Codex plans, Claude reviews; host builds by default |
| claudex-route | Default cross-provider handoff to Codex | Default cross-provider handoff to Claude |
| codex-review | Codex reviews, Claude revises | Claude reviews, Codex revises |
| codex-build | Codex builds, Claude inspects | Claude builds, Codex inspects |
| grill-me-codex | Claude interviews, Codex reviews | Codex interviews, Claude reviews |
| grill-with-docs-codex | Same direction with context, glossary, ADRs | Inverse direction with context, glossary, ADRs |

Codex entry-point names identify Claude as the delegate. The updater maps upstream names to these installed names and retires the old Codex-side copies into inactive backups. Explicit user provider/model choices still take precedence in the general loop and route. The additional grill-by-claude shortcut is intended for a Codex host.

system/scripts/sync-claudex-skills.py updates four upstream support trees and three local entry points through the existing daily Codex automation. It computes separate runtime hashes, inserts runtime directions, and overlays the Codex review/build entry points from system/skill-overrides/claudex/codex. Local aliases remain under system/skill-overrides/claudex. Whole-tree compare-and-swap preserves local edits. Previous copies live outside activation roots under ~/.agents/claudex-backups. No source links or junctions join the vault to the clone.

-Decide which of two files it belongs in. They do different jobs:
+For a replacement, add useful variants in the same pass: the exact error, a trailing-period
+form for one-word dictation, plausible phonetic spellings, joined or split words, and spoken
+punctuation such as "dot" or "at". Add only variants that plausibly identify the intended

-- `replacements.md` fixes a specific mis-hearing. Deterministic, case-insensitive, applied
-  after transcription. This is where almost everything goes.

-A correction with an arrow is a replacement row only. "Save the word X" means a vocabulary
-line (if the model really cannot know it) plus replacement rows for how it gets mangled.
-
-**Think through the variants before writing.** One row per spelling he sends is not enough;
-he sends the next mis-hearing ten minutes later. For each term, write rows for:
-
-- the exact form he sent, lowercase on the heard side
-- the same with a trailing period (`firecrow.`), because a one-word dictation gets
-  auto-punctuated and the rule will not match with the period attached
-- phonetic neighbours: swapped vowels (fisseha → faseya, fuseya, fasaya, fesaya), doubled
-  or dropped letters (firecrawll, firecraw), split or joined words (pre workout, preworkout,
-  cloudcode)
-- spoken forms of anything with punctuation: "at ... dot com", "dot study"
-- a casing pin when the wanted form has specific casing (`pre-workout` → `Pre-workout`,
-  `lsatprep.study` lowercase, `LSATU` → `LSAT U`)
-- the bare surname or bare product name, not only the full email or phrase
-
-Do not add a row whose heard side is an ordinary English word or number ("seven" → Steven,
-"white" → Whit, "care" → Charles, "and review" → blind review). It would fire inside normal
-sentences. When a scan suggests one of those, say so and leave it out.
-
-Note the trade-off on trailing-period rows: a sentence that genuinely ends with the word
-loses its full stop. Say this once when you add the first such row; do not repeat it.
-
-Then run the loop (below). The whole thing is one turn: he sends the word, you reply with
-what you added and the loaded counts. No questions unless the target spelling is ambiguous.
-
-### 2. A mode ("make a mode for ...")
-
-Copy the closest file in `modes/` to a new `modes/<key>.md`. Frontmatter is the config
-(key, name, type, voice_model `sv-1`, language_model, the three context flags, auto_paste);
-the body is the prompt; `## Examples` with `### Input` / `### Output` pairs are appended to
-the prompt text by the sync (the app rejects examples written any other way).

Otherwise bundled skills keep their\n  descriptions, everything else is ranked by usageCount * max(0.5**(days/7), 0.1) and gets its\n  description greedily while budget remains. disable-model-invocation and user-invocable-only skills\n  are not listed at all.\n\nusage: python budget.py [overrides.json]\n\"\"\"\nimport json\nimport sys\nimport time\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport yaml\n\nHOME = Path.home()\nV = HOME / \"dev\" / \"ai-os\"\nCAP = 1536\n\n\ndef load_root(root, prefix=\"\"):\n    out = {}\n    for skf in sorted(root.glob(\"*/SKILL.md\")):\n        text = skf.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\").lstrip(\"﻿\")\n        if not text.startswith(\"---\"):\n            continue\n        fm = yaml.safe_load(text.split(\"---\", 2)[1]) or {}\n        if fm.get(\"disable-model-invocation\") is True or fm.get(\"user-invocable\") is False and False:\n            continue\n        desc = str(fm.get(\"description\") or \"\")\n        if fm.get(\"when_to_use\"):\n            desc += \" - \" + str(fm[\"when_to_use\"])\n        out[prefix + skf.parent.name] = desc\n    return out\n\n\nuser = load_root(HOME / \".claude/skills\")\nproject = {k: v for k, v in load_root(V / \".claude/skills\").items() if k not in user}\nsynced_dirs = list((HOME / \".claude/skills/synced\").glob(\"*\"))\nsynced = load_root(synced_dirs[0], \"anthropic-skills:\") if synced_dirs else {}\nentries = list(user.items()) + list(project.items()) + list(synced.items())\n\nusage = json.load(open(HOME / \".claude.json\", encoding=\"utf-8\"))[\"skillUsage\"]\nnow = time.time() * 1000\n\n\ndef score(name):\n    u = usage.get(name)\n    if not u:\n        return 0.0\n    days = (now - u[\"lastUsedAt\"]) / 86400000\n    return u[\"usageCount\"] * max(0.5 ** (days / 7), 0.1)\n\n\ndef simulate(entries, overrides, budget, fixed):\n    \"\"\"fixed = chars always consumed by bundled/plugin entries outside this model.\"\"\"\n    listed = [(n, d) for n, d in entries if overrides.get(n) not in (\"user-invocable-only\", \"off\")]\n    full = sum(len(n) + 4 + min(len(d), CAP) for n, d in listed) + len(listed) + fixed\n    if full <= budget:\n        return {\"mode\": \"fits\", \"listed\": len(listed), \"described\": [n for n, _ in listed], \"full\": full}\n    names_cost = sum(len(n) + 2 for n, _ in listed) + len(listed) + fixed\n    remaining = budget - names_cost\n    ranked = sorted(((n, d) for n, d in listed if overrides.get(n) != \"name-only\"),\n                    key=lambda nd: -score(nd[0]))  # stable: ties keep listing order\n    described = []\n    for n, d in ranked:\n        cost = min(len(d), CAP) + 2\n        if cost <= remaining:\n            remaining -= cost\n            described.append(n)\n    return {\"mode\": \"priority\", \"listed\": len(listed), \"described\": described, \"full\": full, \"left\": remaining}\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    overrides = json.load(open(sys.argv[1])) if len(sys.argv) > 1 else {}\n    budget = int(sys.argv[2]) if len(sys.argv) > 2 else 40000\n    fixed = int(sys.argv[3]) if len(sys.argv) > 3 else 9000\n    r = simulate(entries, overrides, budget, fixed)\n    print(f\"entries {len(entries)} (user {len(user)}, project-only {len(project)}, synced {len(synced)})\")\n    print(f\"mode {r['mode']}  listed {r['listed']}  described {len(r['described'])}  full-listing chars {r['full']}\")\n    core = (\"pickup wrapup session-handoff audit level-up consultation-inquiry-drafts consultation-followup-drafts \"\n            \"inbound-tutoring-replies transcript-intake post-session-recap guided-drill-assignment \"\n            \"lsat-u-platform-actions tutorocean-profile-fill prompt-architect-refiner accommodations-email \"\n            \"assign-lsatu-work booking-link send-booking-link superwhisper bootcamp-builder lsat-u-voice \"\n            \"lsat-lab-sync morning-brief pull-submissions ingest second-brain-query second-brain-lint\").split()\n    missing = [c for c in core if c not in r[\"described\"]]\n    print(f\"core skills described {len(core) - len(missing)}/{len(core)}; missing: {' '.join(missing)}\")\n    json.dump(r, open(\"budget_out.json\", \"w\"), indent=1)\n"}

{"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os\\38a93a63-e397-478e-9df6-b7085bedd58f\\scratchpad\\scenarios3.py", "content": "\"\"\"Find the smallest budget fraction that describes the whole working set under S3; report listing size.\"\"\"\nimport json\nimport sys\n\nsys.argv = [\"budget.py\"]\nimport budget as B\n\nFIXED = 9000            # bundled/plugin/synced entries outside the local model (calibrated split)\nBASE_EFF = 21200        # effective local budget observed at the default fraction 0.01\nCAP = B.CAP\noverrides = json.load(open(\"s3_overrides.json\"))\ncore_and_used = [n for n, _ in B.entries if overrides.get(n) is None]\n\n\ndef listing_chars(entries, overrides, described):\n    total = 0\n    for n, d in entries:\n        o = overrides.get(n)\n        if o in (\"user-invocable-only\", \"off\"):\n            continue\n        total += len(n) + 3 + ((min(len(d), CAP) + 2) if n in described else 0)\n    return total\n\n\ntoday = B.simulate(B.entries, {}, BASE_EFF + FIXED, FIXED)\nprint(f\"today: local listing {listing_chars(B.entries, {}, set(today['described'])):,} chars, \"\n      f\"{len(today['described'])} described of {today['listed']}\")\nfor frac in (0.01, 0.0125, 0.015, 0.0175, 0.02):\n    eff = int(BASE_EFF + (frac / 0.01 - 1) * (BASE_EFF + FIXED))  # budget scales linearly with the fraction\n    r = B.simulate(B.entries, overrides, eff + FIXED, FIXED)\n    missing = [n for n in core_and_used if n not in r[\"described\"]]\n    print(f\"fraction {frac:<7} mode={r['mode']:<8} described={len(r['described']):<4} \"\n          f\"local listing {listing_chars(B.entries, overrides, set(r['described'])):,} chars; \"\n          f\"working-set skills without description: {len(missing)} {' '.join(missing[:12])}\")\n"}

10	# One-off booking links

12	A URL Joshua sends to one person. She opens it, books one session, pays, and

## user

Tool result:
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Pull booking-time student submissions (files + notes) from the LSAT U backend
# into students/<name>/submissions/. Idempotent: a per-student manifest records
# what has already been pulled, so re-running only fetches new submissions.
#
# Auth: logs in with the admin creds in ~/ai-os/.env against GoTrue (password
# grant), then calls the booking-pass-admin edge function exactly as the admin
# UI does. Files come back as short-lived signed URLs from the private bucket.
#
# Usage: pull-submissions.sh            (pull everything new)
#        pull-submissions.sh --dry-run  (list what would be pulled, write nothing)
set -euo pipefail

AIOS="${AIOS_ROOT:-$HOME/ai-os}"
DRY_RUN=0
[[ "${1:-}" == "--dry-run" ]] && DRY_RUN=1

# --- env: admin creds (root .env) + supabase url/anon (lsatu .env) ------------
set -a
# shellcheck disable=SC1090
source "$AIOS/.env"
# shellcheck disable=SC1091
LSATU_ROOT="${LSATU_ROOT:-$(dirname "$AIOS")/ai-os-projects/lsatu}"
source "$LSATU_ROOT/.env"
set +a

SUPABASE_URL="${VITE_SUPABASE_URL:?missing VITE_SUPABASE_URL}"
ANON="${VITE_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY:?missing VITE_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY}"
EMAIL="${LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL:?missing LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL}"
PASS="${LSATPREP_ADMIN_PASSWORD:?missing LSATPREP_ADMIN_PASSWORD}"

need() { command -v "$1" >/dev/null || { echo "need $1 installed" >&2; exit 1; }; }
need jq; need curl

# --- 1) login -> access token -------------------------------------------------
TOKEN=$(curl -s "$SUPABASE_URL/auth/v1/token?grant_type=password" \
  -H "apikey: [REDACTED] -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d "{\"email\":\"$EMAIL\",\"password\":\"$PASS\"}" | jq -r '.access_token // empty')
[[ -n "$TOKEN" ]] || { echo "login failed" >&2; exit 1; }

## user

Tool result:
1	---
2	name: pull-submissions
3	description: Pull booking-time student submissions (files + comments) from the LSAT U backend into students/<name>/submissions/. Use when Joshua says "pull submissions", "get the booking uploads", "sync student submissions", or as part of /pickup. On-demand only — no cron, no watcher.
4	---
5	
6	## Current AI OS execution contract
7	
8	Resolve current booking mirror paths and relationship IDs from the canonical root/live systems. Use stable source IDs for idempotent filing and do not infer a student identity from a loose name match.
9	
10	# Pull Submissions
11	
12	When a student books a session through their `/book/<slug>` link, the confirm step lets them optionally attach work (any file, up to 5) and leave a comment. Those land in the LSAT U backend (Supabase storage + `external_work_uploads`, comment on `booking_pass_redemptions.student_note`) and are surfaced in the Bookings admin tab and the booking confirmation email to Joshua.
13	
14	This skill is the **on-demand pull** that mirrors those submissions into the ai-os brain, so a student's work lives alongside their transcripts.
15	
16	## What it does
17	
18	1. Logs in with the admin creds in `~/ai-os/.env` (`LSATPREP_ADMIN_*`) against GoTrue, exactly as the admin UI authenticates.
19	2. Calls `booking-pass-admin` → `list_bookings`, keeps every booking that has a note or attached files.
20	3. For each new one, downloads the files (via short-lived signed URLs from `get_booking_uploads`) and writes the comment, into `students/<name>/submissions/`.
21	4. Records each pulled redemption in a per-student `.pulled.json` manifest so re-running only fetches what's new (idempotent).
22	
23	## Run it
24	
25	```bash
26	bash ~/ai-os/skills/claude/pull-submissions/pull-submissions.sh            # pull everything new
27	bash ~/ai-os/skills/claude/pull-submissions/pull-submissions.sh --dry-run  # list only, write nothing
28	```
29	
30	Requires `curl` and `jq`. Reads `VITE_SUPABASE_URL` / `VITE_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` from `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/.env` and the admin creds from the root `.env` (both sourced with absolute paths — cwd is unreliable).
31	
32	## Output layout
33	
34	```
35	students/<name>/submissions/
36	  .pulled.json                       # manifest of redemption ids already pulled
37	  2026-07-19_a1b2c3d4_note.md        # the student's comment (if any)
38	  2026-07-19_a1b2c3d4_PT155.pdf      # each attached file, prefixed date_redemption
39	```
40	
41	Student name → folder slug is derived from the pass name (lowercased, hyphenated). If the folder doesn't exist yet it's created; reconcile a new folder against `students/README.md` naming if the student already has a record under a different slug.
42	
43	## Notes
44	
45	- On-demand by design. Do not add a cron or launchd watcher — Joshua decided the pull is manual (can run inside `/pickup`).
46	- The backend is the source of truth; this is a mirror. Never delete backend rows from here.
47	- Files download through 10-minute signed URLs; if a download 403s, just re-run (a fresh URL is minted each call).
48

10	For drills, messages, student data, access, signups, due dates, unassign, and unsend, use `assign-lsatu-work`. On Windows, invoke its Node entry point directly instead of relying on Bash being on PATH.
11	
12	
13	# LSAT U Platform Actions
14	
15	Joshua's workplace is this brain workspace. LSAT U operations should run headlessly when a supported tool exists. The browser is for checking what a person sees.
16	
17	## Mechanism
18	
19	For an interactive HTML lesson, call the `lsat-u-lessons` MCP tool `publish_interactive_lesson` with the student's exact email, absolute HTML path, and title. The local stdio server is `system/scripts/lsatu-lesson-mcp.mjs`; Codex registration is `lsat-u-lessons`. It calls `system/scripts/lsatu-admin.mjs post-lesson`, which authenticates with the existing admin credentials, posts through `mcp_post_classroom_note`, verifies the saved HTML exactly and student visibility, and then archives only earlier versions with the same lesson marker. A repeat of the current version returns the same item without another notification. Use `dry_run: true` when the student or replacement is uncertain; otherwise publish in one call. MCP tools newly registered during a session may only appear in a new Codex session; the direct Node command is the immediate fallback.
20	
21	The HTML must be a complete document inside AI OS, under 500 KB, with `<meta name="lsatu-interactive-lesson" content="unique-lesson-id">`. Reuse the same lesson ID for revisions of that lesson; use a different ID for a different lesson. Inline the CSS, JavaScript, fonts, and visual assets. Preflight rejects nonembedded resources; do not bypass it or paste HTML into an ordinary sanitized note. The already-live Classroom renderer loads marked HTML in a sandboxed iframe with scripts and forms enabled. **Daily lesson posting is a content operation and never requires Lovable.** Only an actual app renderer change follows the separate app deployment rules.
22	
23	Routine verification is headless: the publisher reads the new item back, compares its HTML byte for byte, checks that it is visible to the intended student, and confirms one active version. The result includes its route and SHA-256. Use Codex's in-app browser only when a visual or interaction defect is suspected or the page design or renderer changed. A teacher login cannot open another student's `/classroom/item/<id>` route directly. Do not claim a student-session visual check unless one was actually performed.
24	
25	The student opens the full lesson from Classroom **Notes and recaps**, the in-app notification, or its Inbox link. Classroom Home may show a recent note as a “Read” row, but it has a four-row limit shared with urgent work, so do not promise that every assigned note stays on Home. This does not affect the note's visibility in Notes and recaps or its direct item route.
26	
27	## UI fallback routing
28	
29	The current student verification route is `/teacher/students/<student-id>/notes`. Its “Posted to their Classroom” section shows active notes and labels archived notes as hidden from the student. Use the browser's normal controls for any unsupported action, then verify the saved state through a fresh read. In Codex, use the in-app browser, not Chrome. Do not rely on old `/teacher?ws=` routes or old browser-tool names.
30	
31	## Order of operations (every action)
32	
33	1. **Identify the student** exactly (roster names include email; ambiguity → one
34	   concise clarifying question). Check `students/<name>/` for context and
35	   `assignment-log.md` to avoid repeats.
36	2. **Do the action** through MCP or the headless Node CLI when supported. Use the UI fallback only for unsupported actions.
37	3. **Verify it landed**: use the publisher's read-after-write result to confirm exact HTML, student visibility, and one active copy of this lesson ID. It archives earlier versions of that ID and leaves unrelated same-title notes alone. Check notifications where available. If verification fails, inspect current items before retrying; never blindly post twice.
38	4. **Log it**: append to `students/<name>/assignment-log.md` (create if missing) with
39	   date, action, item, and evidence; drill verifications also go to
40	   `wiki/guided-drill-verification-log.md`.
41	
42	## Guardrails
43	
44	- Every action here hits **real students on prod**. Anything a student will see
45	  (message text, homework instructions, note bodies) is written in Joshua's voice —
46	  route through `wiki/teaching-voice.md`.
47	- If Joshua's request fully specifies the action ("assign Lydia the conditional
48	  drill"), execute without re-asking. If content is open-ended (a message body he
49	  hasn't dictated), draft it and show him before sending.
50	- Fail closed: if verification is ambiguous, say exactly what was and wasn't
51	  confirmed. Never report an action complete on the strength of a click alone.
52	- No student-facing action as a side effect of a different request.
53

**React controlled-input rule (hard-won 2026-07-16):** `form_input` sets DOM values
but often does NOT update React state, leaving submit buttons silently disabled and
producing zero network traffic. For every form:

1. Click the field and type with real keystrokes (`computer` type action); for
   `<select>`s, click to focus then choose with keyboard (first-letter + Enter) or a
   real option click.
2. Before clicking submit, confirm the button is actually enabled (full-opacity, or
   check `disabled` via read_page).
3. After clicking, confirm via `read_network_requests` that the mutation actually
   fired (POST to `…supabase.co/functions/v1/…` or `/rest/v1/…`) and returned 2xx.
   A UI that merely looks done is not evidence.

1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
2	"""Validate the skills DB model (replaces the old 'expect exactly 11' check).
3	
4	Activation model (changed 2026-07-17, Joshua's call): a project activates a DB skill
5	by COPYING it into `.claude/skills/<name>/` (or `.agents/skills/`), not symlinking.
6	Copies are real files — portable, committable, no dead links on another machine.
7	The tradeoff: a copy does not auto-follow DB edits. This script's (e) check exists
8	because of that tradeoff — it flags copies that have drifted from their DB source.
9	Symlinks are still tolerated (not an error) for any that predate the change or for
10	the unrelated vendor-plugin mechanism in the DB itself (skills/claude/_snapshots
11	pattern), which this script does not touch.
12	
13	Checks:
14	  (a) every activation in an active root resolves to real content (no broken symlink);
15	  (b) no skill lives in an active root un-mirrored (real dir with no DB counterpart);
16	  (c) INDEX.md covers every top-level DB skill;
17	  (d) no duplicate canonical name within a subtree;
18	  (e) a copied (non-symlink) activation's SKILL.md matches its DB source byte-for-byte
19	      — catches silent drift after the DB is edited but the activation isn't re-synced.
20	  (f) every multi-segment path cited in CLAUDE.md / AGENTS.md resolves on disk
21	      — routing maps that point at moved or deleted files send sessions hunting.
22	  (g) no UNDECLARED claude/codex asymmetry. The two subtrees are intentionally unequal,
23	      so the legitimate split is declared in skills/PARITY.md and only new drift fails.
24	  (h) effective Claude/Codex skill availability has no undeclared one-runtime drift;
25	  (i) active instruction scopes have both CLAUDE.md and AGENTS.md;
26	  (j) Codex agent TOML files are valid, complete, and activated from the catalog.
27	Exit 0 = PASS, 1 = FAIL.
28	"""
29	from project_paths import project_path, VAULT_ROOT
30	import os, re, sys, filecmp, tomllib, subprocess

## assistant

Your skills weren't unpopular because they're bad. Most of them couldn't be seen. I found two causes, fixed both, and confirmed the fixes in real Claude Code sessions. Full report: [SUMMARY.md](audits/skills-2026-10-12-karpathy/SUMMARY.md).

## Why unused skills stayed unused

Claude Code only shows the model a skill's description while a budget lasts: 1% of the context window, which is 30,000 characters here. It hands descriptions out by recent use first, then alphabetically. A live debug run measured 403 skills wanting 126,322 characters.

So a skill you've never used gets only its name, can't trigger on its own, and stays unused. `academy-guide` and `brand-guidelines` had full descriptions because they start with A and B. `transcript-intake`, `post-session-recap`, `pickup`, `level-up`, `guided-drill-assignment` and `lsat-u-platform-actions` showed up as bare names. Across 669 past sessions, only 88 skills were ever used.

**Fix:** new settings in the vault's `.claude/settings.json`. The budget goes to 1.5%. 69 vendor skills you've never used (Workspace recipes and personas, enterprise admin tools) are hidden from the model, but still run if you type `/name`. 183 never-used generic skills show by name only.

**Verified in a fresh session:** 10 of 10 sampled core skills now show their descriptions, up from 0. Cost: about 3,750 more tokens per message, in AI OS sessions only.

## Many skills Claude loaded were stale

When a skill exists in both your home folder and the vault, Claude loads the home copy. 142 home copies had drifted from the catalog while the validator still said PASS. Some were actively wrong:

- **superwhisper** said you record with Ctrl+Space. You use Left Alt.
- **wrapup** wrote log entries at the top of the log, in an old format.
- **bootcamp-builder** said to "commit and push without asking" after a green build.
- **lsat-u-platform-actions** still drove Chrome by hand and said there was no backend path.
- **lsat-lab-mega-set** deleted wrong practice sets automatically.

I synced 16 skills from the catalog, after backing up every file and checking that nothing unique would be lost. That check caught a lesson the catalog had dropped, the React form-input rule, so I restored it.

## Other repairs

- **pull-submissions** was broken on Windows. I reproduced the failure first, without touching your backend, then fixed the path. It still needs `jq`, which isn't installed.
- **kling-cli** was missing for Claude. Restored.
- **assign-lsatu-work** had wrong credential notes. Its description is cut from 1,086 to 851 characters, with the trigger phrases kept.
- **Gemini's `audit`** was missing its rubric. Added.
- **The validator** runs on Windows now. It fails when a home copy hides a newer vault copy (tested with a planted stale copy). It no longer skips 26 skills just because `PARITY.md` mentions them.

## Wiki scan

The vault's lint checked 3,896 pages and found 2 dead links, both in old log entries. Four web clips in `raw/` were never ingested. `wiki/tasks.md` hasn't been updated since 2026-10-08.

## What I left for you

1. 121 home copies still differ from the catalog, mostly vendor and generic skills. The validator now shows this count on every run.
2. Install `jq` if you want pull-submissions to work (`winget install jqlang.jq`).
3. Twenty skills on your claude.ai account duplicate local ones, such as `anthropic-skills:pdf`. Removing them is an account change.
4. `level-up` exists in two different designs. Pick one.
5. Ingest the four clips and update `tasks.md` at the next wrapup.

Midway through, another session installed 36 Expo and SEO skills that you'd authorized. I didn't touch them. They would have pushed three of your core skills back to bare names. Expo's own `expo-overview` skill is designed to route to its sub-skills, so those sub-skills now show by name only.

## Assumptions

- "Never used" means on this laptop. MacBook sessions aren't in these logs, and any name-only setting is easy to undo.
- I verified Claude's loading behavior. Codex's wasn't tested; I made every copy identical so it shouldn't matter.
- I didn't use the karpathy subagent. This was a repair of your skill system, not a teaching question, so I applied the method myself and graded the work against the seven rules in `wiki/karpathy/rules.md`.

## Seven-rule grades

1. **Mechanism:** pass. The budget rules and load order come from the installed Claude Code program, confirmed in live runs.
2. **Small baseline:** pass. I measured before changing anything and reproduced single failures first.
3. **Predict and compare:** pass. Live runs matched every prediction, except one I logged: I expected YAML errors and found none.
4. **Counterexample:** pass. Two of my own bugs were caught and fixed, including a rule that would have hidden your `persona-agent-creator`.
5. **Verified behavior:** pass for Claude. The proof is what the running app loaded. The Codex runtime wasn't tested.
6. **Assumptions:** pass. Listed above.
7. **Simplicity:** pass. I used Claude Code's own settings plus two small scripts.

## What I ran

A scan of 1,910 skill copies and 669 session logs, three headless Claude runs with debug logging, a before-and-after test of pull-submissions, the validator plus a planted failure, and the wiki lint.

## What came out

The validator passes (311 Claude, 299 Codex, 222 Gemini skills) with drift warnings. A rerun of the sync makes zero changes. All sampled core skills are described.

## What I changed

The vault's listing settings, the validator, six catalog skills, 16 home-folder skills, `kling-cli`, Gemini's `audit`, the skills README, a log entry and two memory notes. Nothing deleted, sent or committed.

## user

Tool result:
  -d "{\"email\":\"$EMAIL\",\"password\":\"$PASS\"}" | jq -r '.access_token // empty')
[[ -n "$TOKEN" ]] || { echo "login failed" >&2; exit 1; }

admin() { # action json -> response
  curl -s "$SUPABASE_URL/functions/v1/booking-pass-admin" \
    -H "Authorization: [REDACTED] $TOKEN" -H "apikey: [REDACTED] -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
    -d "$1"
}

slugify() { echo "$1" | tr '[:upper:]' '[:lower:]' | sed -E 's/[^a-z0-9]+/-/g; s/^-+|-+$//g'; }

# --- 2) list bookings, keep those with a note or files ------------------------
BOOKINGS=$(admin '{"action":"list_bookings"}' | jq -c '[.bookings[] | select((.upload_count // 0) > 0 or (.student_note // "" | length > 0))]')
COUNT=$(echo "$BOOKINGS" | jq 'length')
echo "Found $COUNT booking(s) with a submission."

pulled_new=0
for row in $(echo "$BOOKINGS" | jq -c '.[]'); do
  RID=$(echo "$row" | jq -r '.id')
  NOTE=$(echo "$row" | jq -r '.student_note // ""')
  NUP=$(echo "$row" | jq -r '.upload_count // 0')
  START=$(echo "$row" | jq -r '.start_at')
  NAME=$(echo "$row" | jq -r '.booking_passes.student_name // .student_name_snapshot // .booking_passes.student_email // .student_email_snapshot // "unknown-student"')
  DATE=$(echo "$START" | cut -c1-10)
  SLUG=$(slugify "$NAME")

  DEST="$AIOS/students/$SLUG/submissions"
  MANIFEST="$DEST/.pulled.json"
  # already pulled this redemption?
  if [[ -f "$MANIFEST" ]] && jq -e --arg r "$RID" 'index($r)' "$MANIFEST" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    continue
  fi

  echo "  • $NAME ($DATE): note=$([[ -n "$NOTE" ]] && echo yes || echo no) files=$NUP"
  [[ $DRY_RUN -eq 1 ]] && continue

  mkdir -p "$DEST"
  BASE="$DEST/${DATE}_${RID:0:8}"

  # note file
  if [[ -n "$NOTE" ]]; then
    { echo "# Booking submission note — $NAME"; echo; echo "- Session: $START"; echo "- Redemption: $RID"; echo; echo "$NOTE"; } > "${BASE}_note.md"
  fi

  # files via signed urls
  if [[ "$NUP" -gt 0 ]]; then
    UPLOADS=$(admin "{\"action\":\"get_booking_uploads\",\"redemption_id\":\"$RID\"}")
    for u in $(echo "$UPLOADS" | jq -c '.uploads[]'); do
      FNAME=$(echo "$u" | jq -r '.file_name')
      URL=$(echo "$u" | jq -r '.url // empty')
      [[ -n "$URL" ]] || { echo "    (no url for $FNAME)"; continue; }
      curl -s -L -o "${BASE}_${FNAME}" "$URL" && echo "    ↓ ${DATE}_${RID:0:8}_${FNAME}"
    done
  fi

  # record in manifest
  if [[ -f "$MANIFEST" ]]; then
    jq --arg r "$RID" '. + [$r] | unique' "$MANIFEST" > "$MANIFEST.tmp" && mv "$MANIFEST.tmp" "$MANIFEST"
  else
    echo "[\"$RID\"]" > "$MANIFEST"
  fi
  pulled_new=$((pulled_new+1))
done

echo "Done. Pulled $pulled_new new submission(s)."
